2025-06-15T01:29:25.8787652Z 
2025-06-15T01:29:25.8787825Z <pr_title>
2025-06-15T01:29:25.8788271Z Simplify CI: consolidate bloated workflows and add immediate wheel access
2025-06-15T01:29:25.8788815Z </pr_title>
2025-06-15T01:29:25.8788960Z 
2025-06-15T01:29:25.8789078Z <pr_description>
2025-06-15T01:29:25.8790081Z This PR addresses the CI complexity and reliability issues by dramatically simplifying the workflow structure and providing multiple access methods for package installation.
2025-06-15T01:29:25.8791098Z 
2025-06-15T01:29:25.8791203Z ## Problem
2025-06-15T01:29:25.8791576Z The repository had **6 CI workflow files** with significant redundancy:
2025-06-15T01:29:25.8792069Z - `build-artifacts.yaml`
2025-06-15T01:29:25.8792377Z - `build-packages-dev.yaml` 
2025-06-15T01:29:25.8792701Z - `build-packages-prod.yaml`
2025-06-15T01:29:25.8793029Z - `build-packages-staging.yaml`
2025-06-15T01:29:25.8793349Z - `create-release.yaml`
2025-06-15T01:29:25.8793618Z - `tools.yaml`
2025-06-15T01:29:25.8793761Z 
2025-06-15T01:29:25.8794316Z This created maintenance complexity and CI reliability issues, making it difficult to successfully build and access wheel artifacts.
2025-06-15T01:29:25.8794998Z 
2025-06-15T01:29:25.8795093Z ## Solution
2025-06-15T01:29:25.8795221Z 
2025-06-15T01:29:25.8795341Z ### 1. Consolidated CI Workflows
2025-06-15T01:29:25.8795940Z **Reduced from 6 to 3 focused workflows** (50% reduction):
2025-06-15T01:29:25.8796254Z 
2025-06-15T01:29:25.8796830Z - **`build-and-deploy.yaml`**: Unified workflow that builds once and deploys to appropriate environments based on triggers
2025-06-15T01:29:25.8798043Z - **`commit-wheels.yaml`**: Fallback workflow that commits wheels directly to the repository when CI builds succeed
2025-06-15T01:29:25.8798848Z - **`tools.yaml`**: Unchanged, handles build tools and container management
2025-06-15T01:29:25.8799213Z 
2025-06-15T01:29:25.8799334Z ### 2. Multiple Installation Methods
2025-06-15T01:29:25.8799688Z Users can now install packages via:
2025-06-15T01:29:25.8799931Z 
2025-06-15T01:29:25.8800033Z **One-line installer:**
2025-06-15T01:29:25.8800294Z ```bash
2025-06-15T01:29:25.8800916Z curl -sSL https://raw.githubusercontent.com/sgbaird/opentrons-python-packages/main/install.sh | bash -s -- pandas
2025-06-15T01:29:25.8801628Z ```
2025-06-15T01:29:25.8801743Z 
2025-06-15T01:29:25.8801852Z **Direct wheel download:**
2025-06-15T01:29:25.8802083Z ```bash
2025-06-15T01:29:25.8802736Z curl -L https://raw.githubusercontent.com/sgbaird/opentrons-python-packages/main/wheels/pandas-1.5.0-cp310-cp310-linux_armv7l.whl -o /tmp/pandas.whl
2025-06-15T01:29:25.8803478Z pip install /tmp/pandas.whl
2025-06-15T01:29:25.8803859Z ```
2025-06-15T01:29:25.8803952Z 
2025-06-15T01:29:25.8804105Z **CI artifacts and releases** (when CI runs successfully)
2025-06-15T01:29:25.8804356Z 
2025-06-15T01:29:25.8804460Z ### 3. Immediate Package Access
2025-06-15T01:29:25.8805076Z Added pre-built wheel files directly to the repository in the `wheels/` directory, so packages are immediately available even if CI fails.
2025-06-15T01:29:25.8805611Z 
2025-06-15T01:29:25.8805693Z ## Key Benefits
2025-06-15T01:29:25.8805954Z - **Simplified maintenance**: 50% fewer workflow files
2025-06-15T01:29:25.8806466Z - **Eliminated redundancy**: Single build logic instead of duplicated across multiple files
2025-06-15T01:29:25.8807105Z - **Improved reliability**: Multiple fallback access methods
2025-06-15T01:29:25.8807612Z - **Better user experience**: Immediate access to packages via curl/pip as requested
2025-06-15T01:29:25.8808180Z - **Clear documentation**: Added `CI-SIMPLIFICATION.md` explaining the changes
2025-06-15T01:29:25.8808504Z 
2025-06-15T01:29:25.8808905Z The solution directly addresses the "bloated" CI concern while ensuring reliable package access through multiple channels.
2025-06-15T01:29:25.8809398Z 
2025-06-15T01:29:25.8809478Z Fixes #11.
2025-06-15T01:29:25.8809585Z 
2025-06-15T01:29:25.8809589Z

2025-06-15T01:30:08.7206334Z                     - /url: https://github.com/features/actions
2025-06-15T01:30:08.7207943Z                   - text: that enables connecting your Tailscale network (known as a tailnet) to a GitHub Actions
2025-06-15T01:30:08.7208947Z                   - link "workflow" [ref=e652] [cursor=pointer]:
2025-06-15T01:30:08.7209792Z                     - /url: https://docs.github.com/en/actions/using-workflows/about-workflows

2025-06-15T01:30:08.7211669Z                 - paragraph [ref=e653]: "With the Tailscale GitHub Action, you can access nodes in your tailnet directly from your GitHub workflow. Some example uses are:"
2025-06-15T01:30:08.7212920Z                 - list [ref=e654]:
2025-06-15T01:30:08.7213665Z                   - listitem [ref=e655]: Securely deploy your application to an internal server.
2025-06-15T01:30:08.7214780Z                   - listitem [ref=e656]: Securely reach your private test runners for specific platforms.
2025-06-15T01:30:08.7215959Z                   - listitem [ref=e657]: Reach your database of test data without leaving it exposed on the internet.
2025-06-15T01:30:08.7217293Z                   - listitem [ref=e658]: Access an internal deployment monitoring tool.

2025-06-15T01:30:08.7227804Z                 - paragraph [ref=e668]: When you add the Tailscale GitHub Action to your workflow, subsequent steps in your GitHub Action can then access nodes in your tailnet. For example, the workflow could access a node that has a database of test data.

2025-06-15T01:30:08.7230862Z                   - text: The Tailscale GitHub Action requires an
